People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah... I guess I came in because things have felt harder than they should. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.

I’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.

It’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.

So no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.

Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.

It’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It kind of started when I decided I was going to overhaul my routine and how I present myself—like, clothes, schedule, just trying to seem more put-together. I thought it would be motivating, but around then is when the tense, low feeling really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. So I don’t know if I’m enjoying them less, or if I’m just making them weirdly stressful lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um… I think so? I mean, I still get some pleasure from small things, like arranging colors or looking at outfits online, but then I start comparing things or thinking I should be using that time better.\n\nLike I was looking at fabric swatches the other day and somehow ended up reading about booth displays for this craft market I’m not even sure I’m going to apply to, and then I forgot why I started. So… yes, kind of, but it gets tangled up pretty fast.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel fully engaged and able to enjoy them without getting distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Not really fully, I guess. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.

Some days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. But tired in a new or really obvious way? I’m not sure I can say that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I guess I have been feeling guilty, mostly about not keeping up with things I normally would.

Like, I came in because I was trying to sort of clean up my image and my routine for the arts board stuff, and instead of it making me feel more together, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F43.23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess I came in because things have felt harder than they should. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It kind of started when I decided I was going to overhaul my routine and how I present myself—like, clothes, schedule, just trying to seem more put-together. I thought it would be motivating, but around then is when the tense, low feeling really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. So I don’t know if I’m enjoying them less, or if I’m just making them weirdly stressful lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um… I think so? I mean, I still get some pleasure from small things, like arranging colors or looking at outfits online, but then I start comparing things or thinking I should be using that time better.\n\nLike I was looking at fabric swatches the other day and somehow ended up reading about booth displays for this craft market I’m not even sure I’m going to apply to, and then I forgot why I started. So… yes, kind of, but it gets tangled up pretty fast.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel fully engaged and able to enjoy them without getting distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Not really fully, I guess. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. But tired in a new or really obvious way? I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been feeling guilty, mostly about not keeping up with things I normally would.\n\nLike, I came in because I was trying to sort of clean up my image and my routine for the arts board stuff, and instead of it making me feel more together, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F43.23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess I came in because things have felt harder than they should. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It kind of started when I decided I was going to overhaul my routine and how I present myself—like, clothes, schedule, just trying to seem more put-together. I thought it would be motivating, but around then is when the tense, low feeling really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. So I don’t know if I’m enjoying them less, or if I’m just making them weirdly stressful lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um… I think so? I mean, I still get some pleasure from small things, like arranging colors or looking at outfits online, but then I start comparing things or thinking I should be using that time better.\n\nLike I was looking at fabric swatches the other day and somehow ended up reading about booth displays for this craft market I’m not even sure I’m going to apply to, and then I forgot why I started. So… yes, kind of, but it gets tangled up pretty fast.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel fully engaged and able to enjoy them without getting distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Not really fully, I guess. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess I came in because things have felt harder than they should. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It kind of started when I decided I was going to overhaul my routine and how I present myself—like, clothes, schedule, just trying to seem more put-together. I thought it would be motivating, but around then is when the tense, low feeling really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. So I don’t know if I’m enjoying them less, or if I’m just making them weirdly stressful lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um… I think so? I mean, I still get some pleasure from small things, like arranging colors or looking at outfits online, but then I start comparing things or thinking I should be using that time better.\n\nLike I was looking at fabric swatches the other day and somehow ended up reading about booth displays for this craft market I’m not even sure I’m going to apply to, and then I forgot why I started. So… yes, kind of, but it gets tangled up pretty fast.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel fully engaged and able to enjoy them without getting distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Not really fully, I guess. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say I feel like a failure all the time… but I do get this feeling like I’m not keeping up with who I’m supposed to be, if that makes sense.

Like, I came in because since I tried to clean up my image and get a better routine for the arts board stuff, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. And it’s weird because I’m still doing the basics, like I’m not completely falling apart, but then I’ll drop these little things I normally care about—fair planning notes, design drafts, stuff like that—and then I feel guilty.

And then instead of actually catching up, I’ll cancel plans because I think, okay, I need that time to fix everything, but then I don’t always fix it. So then it feels like I’ve failed twice, even if that sounds dramatic.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F43.23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess I came in because things have felt harder than they should. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It kind of started when I decided I was going to overhaul my routine and how I present myself—like, clothes, schedule, just trying to seem more put-together. I thought it would be motivating, but around then is when the tense, low feeling really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. So I don’t know if I’m enjoying them less, or if I’m just making them weirdly stressful lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um… I think so? I mean, I still get some pleasure from small things, like arranging colors or looking at outfits online, but then I start comparing things or thinking I should be using that time better.\n\nLike I was looking at fabric swatches the other day and somehow ended up reading about booth displays for this craft market I’m not even sure I’m going to apply to, and then I forgot why I started. So… yes, kind of, but it gets tangled up pretty fast.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel fully engaged and able to enjoy them without getting distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Not really fully, I guess. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. But tired in a new or really obvious way? I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been feeling guilty, mostly about not keeping up with things I normally would.\n\nLike, I came in because I was trying to sort of clean up my image and my routine for the arts board stuff, and instead of it making me feel more together, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. I’m still doing the basic things, but I keep dropping little things I actually care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts I meant to work on—and then I feel bad about it.\n\nAnd then I’ll cancel plans because I feel like I should be catching up instead, but then I don’t really catch up either, so it just… kind of makes me feel worse. Not worthless exactly, but guilty, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death lately?\n\nPatient: Thoughts about death… um, not really that I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll get kind of overwhelmed and my brain goes into that “what am I even doing with myself” loop, but I don’t think I’ve been sitting around thinking about death specifically. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F43.23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess I came in because things have felt harder than they should. I’ve been trying to clean up my image and get my routine together for some arts board stuff, and I thought that would make me feel more organized, but instead I’ve just been kind of tense and down.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, mostly, but I keep dropping little stuff I normally care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts—and then I feel really guilty about it. And instead of catching up, I end up canceling plans, which probably makes it worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling tense and down?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It kind of started when I decided I was going to overhaul my routine and how I present myself—like, clothes, schedule, just trying to seem more put-together. I thought it would be motivating, but around then is when the tense, low feeling really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. So I don’t know if I’m enjoying them less, or if I’m just making them weirdly stressful lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um… I think so? I mean, I still get some pleasure from small things, like arranging colors or looking at outfits online, but then I start comparing things or thinking I should be using that time better.\n\nLike I was looking at fabric swatches the other day and somehow ended up reading about booth displays for this craft market I’m not even sure I’m going to apply to, and then I forgot why I started. So… yes, kind of, but it gets tangled up pretty fast.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel fully engaged and able to enjoy them without getting distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Not really fully, I guess. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. But tired in a new or really obvious way? I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been feeling guilty, mostly about not keeping up with things I normally would.\n\nLike, I came in because I was trying to sort of clean up my image and my routine for the arts board stuff, and instead of it making me feel more together, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. I’m still doing the basic things, but I keep dropping little things I actually care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts I meant to work on—and then I feel bad about it.\n\nAnd then I’ll cancel plans because I feel like I should be catching up instead, but then I don’t really catch up either, so it just… kind of makes me feel worse. Not worthless exactly, but guilty, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death lately?\n\nPatient: Thoughts about death… um, not really that I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll get kind of overwhelmed and my brain goes into that “what am I even doing with myself” loop, but I don’t think I’ve been sitting around thinking about death specifically. It’s more like… I get stuck on whether I’m messing things up, and then suddenly I’m reorganizing a drawer or looking at old project folders for no real reason.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think death has been on my mind lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get really frustrated with myself sometimes, and I’ll kind of spiral about how I’m not doing things the way I’m supposed to, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and overthink something stupid, like whether I answered an email the right way, and then suddenly I’m rearranging my desk instead of actually dealing with it. But harming myself, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Not really worthless, no… I mean, I feel bad about myself sometimes, but it’s more guilt than “I’m worthless,” if that makes sense.\n\nLike since I tried to clean up my image and get my routine together for the arts board stuff, I’ve been way more tense and down than I expected. Like, I still like the idea of those things—going to art fairs, sketching, putting outfits together—but sometimes when I actually sit down to do them, I get distracted or start thinking about whether I’m doing it “right.” \n\nLike last week I was trying to work on a design draft and ended up reorganizing my pins for an hour, which is… not exactly the same thing. I can start something like a design draft or planning for a fair and I want to enjoy it, but then I get tense about whether I’m doing it the right way or whether it fits this more “put-together” image I’ve been trying to have.\n\nAnd then I’ll drop some small thing I normally care about, like notes I meant to make, and feel guilty. Sometimes instead of catching up I just cancel plans, which is frustrating because I’m still doing the basics, I’m not completely falling apart. It’s just… I don’t feel absorbed in things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything I’d say I don’t enjoy at all now. It’s more like… I still want to do them, and sometimes I do enjoy parts of them, but I get sidetracked or start overthinking.\n\nLike even with sketching, I’ll still like the texture of the pencil or picking colors—sorry, that sounds weird—but then I’ll end up thinking about whether it would look good enough to show someone. So I wouldn’t say the enjoyment is gone completely. It’s just not as easy to stay in it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you can't seem to derive any pleasure from them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. Like, I can’t think of something I used to love where now there’s absolutely nothing there.\n\nIt’s more… the pleasure is kind of interrupted, if that makes sense. I’ll start doing something and there are still little parts I like, but then my mind goes off somewhere else. Like I’ll be thinking about colors and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether my desk looks too messy, or if I should reorganize everything, and then I’ve lost the thread.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s anything I get no pleasure from at all. It’s just harder to stay with it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you feel you've completely lost interest?\n\nPatient: Completely lost interest… I don’t think so. Not in a clean, “I don’t care about this anymore” way.\n\nIt’s more like I drift away from things, or I get caught up in whether I’m doing them right. Like I’ll think I want to work on something creative, and then somehow I’m rearranging supplies or thinking about how it should look, and then the actual thing doesn’t really happen. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. But tired in a new or really obvious way? I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been feeling guilty, mostly about not keeping up with things I normally would.\n\nLike, I came in because I was trying to sort of clean up my image and my routine for the arts board stuff, and instead of it making me feel more together, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. I’m still doing the basic things, but I keep dropping little things I actually care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts I meant to work on—and then I feel bad about it.\n\nAnd then I’ll cancel plans because I feel like I should be catching up instead, but then I don’t really catch up either, so it just… kind of makes me feel worse. Not worthless exactly, but guilty, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death lately?\n\nPatient: Thoughts about death… um, not really that I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll get kind of overwhelmed and my brain goes into that “what am I even doing with myself” loop, but I don’t think I’ve been sitting around thinking about death specifically. It’s more like… I get stuck on whether I’m messing things up, and then suddenly I’m reorganizing a drawer or looking at old project folders for no real reason.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think death has been on my mind lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get really frustrated with myself sometimes, and I’ll kind of spiral about how I’m not doing things the way I’m supposed to, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and overthink something stupid, like whether I answered an email the right way, and then suddenly I’m rearranging my desk instead of actually dealing with it. But harming myself, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Not really worthless, no… I mean, I feel bad about myself sometimes, but it’s more guilt than “I’m worthless,” if that makes sense.\n\nLike since I tried to clean up my image and get my routine together for the arts board stuff, I’ve been way more tense and down than I expected. I’m still doing the basic things, but then I’ll forget or drop little things I actually care about, like fair planning notes or design drafts, and then I feel guilty. And then I cancel plans because I think I should catch up, but I don’t always catch up, so it just turns into this whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're a failure?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I feel like a failure all the time… but I do get this feeling like I’m not keeping up with who I’m supposed to be, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I came in because since I tried to clean up my image and get a better routine for the arts board stuff, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. And it’s weird because I’m still doing the basics, like I’m not completely falling apart, but then I’ll drop these little things I normally care about—fair planning notes, design drafts, stuff like that—and then I feel guilty.\n\nAnd then instead of actually catching up, I’ll cancel plans because I think, okay, I need that time to fix everything, but then I don’t always fix it. So then it feels like I’ve failed twice, even if that sounds dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not anything I’ve really noticed. My clothes fit about the same, I think.\n\nI mean, I haven’t been weighing myself regularly—honestly the scale at home got shoved behind some laundry baskets, and then I kept meaning to organize that whole corner, but it turned into one of those things where I start sorting socks instead. But no, I wouldn’t say there’s been a significant weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your actions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that exactly. Like, I don’t think people have told me I’m moving weirdly slow or anything, and I don’t feel like I’m rushing around all sped up.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of stall on starting things, but then I get distracted by, like, reorganizing one pile of papers instead of doing the thing I meant to do… but that’s not really moving slower, I guess. So, no, not a big change that I can point to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But I don’t feel like the interest is gone. I still kind of want to want to do it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I haven’t really been tracking it closely. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I guess.\n\nI mean, my scale at home is kind of unreliable anyway—it’s one of those old ones that gives a different number if you move it slightly on the floor—so I don’t really trust it. But no, I haven’t noticed any weight loss recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that really stands out to me. I mean, I have nights where I get distracted and end up staying up later than I meant to, but that’s also kind of always been me if I get pulled into something, like organizing tabs on my computer or looking up one thing and then it becomes five things.\n\nBut trouble sleeping, like lying there unable to sleep every night or waking up all the time… I haven’t really noticed that as a big issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: More tired than usual… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nSome days I feel kind of drained, but then I think that might just be from getting stuck in little things all day—like I’ll spend way too long deciding where something should go, or I’ll start cleaning one corner and somehow it becomes this whole thing. But tired in a new or really obvious way? I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been feeling guilty, mostly about not keeping up with things I normally would.\n\nLike, I came in because I was trying to sort of clean up my image and my routine for the arts board stuff, and instead of it making me feel more together, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. I’m still doing the basic things, but I keep dropping little things I actually care about—like fair planning notes, or design drafts I meant to work on—and then I feel bad about it.\n\nAnd then I’ll cancel plans because I feel like I should be catching up instead, but then I don’t really catch up either, so it just… kind of makes me feel worse. Not worthless exactly, but guilty, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death lately?\n\nPatient: Thoughts about death… um, not really that I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll get kind of overwhelmed and my brain goes into that “what am I even doing with myself” loop, but I don’t think I’ve been sitting around thinking about death specifically. It’s more like… I get stuck on whether I’m messing things up, and then suddenly I’m reorganizing a drawer or looking at old project folders for no real reason.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think death has been on my mind lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get really frustrated with myself sometimes, and I’ll kind of spiral about how I’m not doing things the way I’m supposed to, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll sit there and overthink something stupid, like whether I answered an email the right way, and then suddenly I’m rearranging my desk instead of actually dealing with it. But harming myself, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Not really worthless, no… I mean, I feel bad about myself sometimes, but it’s more guilt than “I’m worthless,” if that makes sense.\n\nLike since I tried to clean up my image and get my routine together for the arts board stuff, I’ve been way more tense and down than I expected. I’m still doing the basic things, but then I’ll forget or drop little things I actually care about, like fair planning notes or design drafts, and then I feel guilty. And then I cancel plans because I think I should catch up, but I don’t always catch up, so it just turns into this whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're a failure?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I feel like a failure all the time… but I do get this feeling like I’m not keeping up with who I’m supposed to be, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I came in because since I tried to clean up my image and get a better routine for the arts board stuff, I’ve just been more tense and kind of down than I expected. So then it feels like I’ve failed twice, even if that sounds dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not anything I’ve really noticed. My clothes fit about the same, I think.\n\nI mean, I haven’t been weighing myself regularly—honestly the scale at home got shoved behind some laundry baskets, and then I kept meaning to organize that whole corner, but it turned into one of those things where I start sorting socks instead. But no, I wouldn’t say there’s been a significant weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your actions lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that exactly. Like, I don’t think people have told me I’m moving weirdly slow or anything, and I don’t feel like I’m rushing around all sped up.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of stall on starting things, but then I get distracted by, like, reorganizing one pile of papers instead of doing the thing I meant to do… but that’s not really moving slower, I guess. So, no, not a big change that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a way I’ve really noticed. Like, I might sit there longer before I get going on something, but once I’m up and doing stuff, I don’t feel like I’m physically slower.\n\nThough now I’m thinking about how yesterday I spent, like, twenty minutes looking for the good scissors instead of just using the regular ones, which probably made everything take longer—but that’s more me getting sidetracked than moving slowly, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a distinct period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most activities?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a really distinct two-week thing exactly… It’s more like since I tried to clean up my image and routine for the arts board stuff, I’ve been more tense and kind of down than I expected.\n\nI’m still doing the basic things, like I’m not totally stopping everything, but I keep dropping little things I normally care about—like fair planning notes or design draft ideas—and then I feel guilty and cancel plans instead of just catching up.
